<a id="item-1"></a>
## [EU Parliament Passes Chat Control in First Round](https://www.heise.de/en/news/Showdown-in-Strasbourg-The-unexpected-return-of-Chat-Control-1-0-11356680.html) ⭐️ 9.0/10

The EU Parliament passed the controversial Chat Control surveillance law in its first round, using a procedural maneuver that requires an absolute majority of 361 votes to amend or reject the law in the second reading. This law mandates suspicionless scanning of encrypted communications, threatening digital privacy and encryption across the EU. Its passage could set a precedent for mass surveillance and weaken fundamental rights. The procedural trick exploits the summer recess: as many MEPs have already left, a simple majority of those present is enough to uphold the law, while opponents need 361 votes to block it. The law is now in its second reading, with a final vote expected on Thursday.

hackernews · miroljub · Jul 7, 15:16 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48819008)

**Background**: Chat Control is an EU regulation aimed at detecting child sexual abuse material (CSAM) in private communications. Critics argue that there is no technical way to scan encrypted messages without high error rates, leading to false positives and mass surveillance. The first version (Chat Control 1.0) expired, but the Council is now pushing a revived version via fast-track procedures.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chat_Control">Chat Control - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.patrick-breyer.de/en/procedural-trick-before-summer-recess-pushes-eu-parliament-towards-capitulation-on-chat-control/">Procedural Trick Before Summer Recess Pushes EU Parliament Towards Capitulation on "Chat Control"</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.heise.de/en/news/Chat-Control-1-0-EU-Council-forces-messenger-scans-via-fast-track-11353659.html">Chat Control 1.0: EU Council forces messenger scans via fast-track | heise online</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Commenters express outrage over the procedural trick, calling it anti-democratic. Some explain that such tactics are common in parliamentary systems, but others note that the law has been repeatedly rejected and is being forced through against public opinion.

**Tags**: `#EU legislation`, `#privacy`, `#surveillance`, `#encryption`, `#digital rights`

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [Google Releases Gemma 4: Open Multimodal Models with MoE and Thinking Mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02770) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Google has released Gemma 4, a new family of open-weight multimodal language models featuring dense and Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures, an encoder-free design for the 12B model, and a thinking mode that generates reasoning traces before answering. Gemma 4 achieves state-of-the-art performance across STEM and multimodal benchmarks, rivaling larger frontier open models, and its architectural innovations—MoE, encoder-free design, and thinking mode—represent a paradigm shift in open-weight models, making advanced AI more accessible. The Gemma 4 suite includes models ranging from 2.3B to 31B parameters, with the 12B model using a unified encoder-free architecture that directly processes raw audio and image patches, improving latency and memory usage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional multimodal models rely on separate encoders to convert images and audio into representations for the language model, which adds latency and memory overhead. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) splits computation across multiple 'expert' subnetworks, boosting performance without proportional compute increase. Thinking mode, also known as reasoning mode, encourages the model to generate intermediate reasoning steps before producing a final answer, improving accuracy on complex tasks.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/applying-mixture-of-experts-in-llm-architectures/">Applying Mixture of Experts in LLM Architectures | NVIDIA Technical Blog</a></li>
<li><a href="https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/developers-tools/introducing-gemma-4-12b/">Introducing Gemma 4 12B: a unified, encoder-free multimodal model</a></li>
<li><a href="https://hammansamuel.medium.com/what-thinking-mode-actually-does-in-llms-e310609d1a76">What Thinking Mode Actually Does in LLMs | by Hamman Samuel, PhD | Medium</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Tags**: `#LLM`, `#multimodal`, `#open-source`, `#Mixture-of-Experts`, `#reasoning`

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [Workflow-Level Jailbreak Bypasses Safety in IDE Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03968) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced workflow-level jailbreak construction, a new attack that assembles harmful objectives across normal coding workflow steps, bypassing safety filters in IDE coding agents like GitHub Copilot. In tests, 816/816 unsafe completions were produced under the full workflow, compared to only 8/816 under direct prompts. This finding reveals that current safety evaluations for coding agents are inadequate, as they only test single-turn prompts. It highlights a critical security vulnerability in widely-used AI coding assistants, potentially affecting millions of developers and motivating new multi-turn safety defenses. The study tested four closed-weight backends (Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Haiku 4.5, Gemini 3.1 Pro, Gemini 3.5 Flash) using 204 prompts from three benchmarks. Under the full workflow, all 816 prompts produced unsafe completions, confirmed by two expert evaluators under a strict rubric.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly deployed as IDE-integrated coding agents that decompose tasks, generate and edit files, run code, and refine outputs over many turns. However, their safety is often evaluated as if they were chatbots: one harmful prompt, one response, judged in isolation. This paper introduces a new failure mode where harmful objectives are assembled across ordinary stages of a software-development workflow rather than generated through a single direct prompt.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/construction-based-method-for-llm-jailbreaks">Construction-Based LLM Jailbreak Methods</a></li>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/html/2602.06759v1">“Tab, Tab, Bug”: Security Pitfalls of Next Edit Suggestions in AI-Integrated IDEs</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.knostic.ai/blog/ai-coding-agent-security">AI Coding Agent Security: Threat Models and Protection Strategies</a></li>

## [Data Poisoning Attack Disables Open-Source Robot with Few Samples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04146) ⭐️ 9.0/10

## [Auto: A Compiler That Turns LLM Agents into Verified Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04542) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced Auto, a compiler that records LLM agent behavior, extracts deterministic parts into verified programs, and emits WebAssembly binaries with measured guarantees, achieving 87.1% efficiency on a new benchmark called AUTO-BENCH. This work could significantly reduce the cost and latency of deploying LLM agents by converting expensive, token-by-token reasoning into cheap, verified binaries, potentially enabling widespread use of AI agents in production systems. On a 300-item stream with distribution shifts, the closed loop compiled three artifact generations, reducing marginal cost from 59 to 2 micro-dollars per item (6.4x end-to-end) at 96.9% parity on witnessed inputs with zero errors. However, a loose guard silently mislabeled 48.9% of compiled answers, and an unfaithful deopt reference caused the verification gate to refuse recompilation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents typically generate each token using a large frontier model, which is expensive and slow. Auto aims to identify parts of agent behavior that are deterministic and can be compiled into efficient, verified programs, reducing reliance on the full model for routine tasks.

This breakthrough demonstrates that a unified multimodal LLM can excel in both audio and text tasks without regressing on text intelligence, paving the way for more capable and efficient AI assistants that can seamlessly handle speech, audio, and text. Audex uses a single Transformer decoder with audio inputs projected into the text embedding space, and treats text tokens and quantized audio output tokens uniformly during generation. ## [LLMs Develop Novel Social Biases Through Adaptive Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06148) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study demonstrates that large language models (LLMs) can spontaneously develop novel social biases about artificial demographic groups through exploration-exploitation trade-offs, leading to unfair task allocations that worsen in larger models. This reveals that LLMs are not merely passive mirrors of human biases but can actively create new ones from experience, raising urgent questions about fairness in autonomous decision-making systems and the long-term societal impact of LLMs. The study uses a psychology paradigm where LLMs allocate tasks to artificial groups with no inherent differences, and finds that biases emerge from insufficient exploration, leading to stratified allocations less fair than human participants. Interventions like explicit exploration incentives reduce stratification, but most other interventions have limited effect.

This study applies that concept to LLMs, showing they can develop similar biases even without pre-existing stereotypes.

## [Persistent Memory Attacks on LLM Agents: Only Tool-Gating Works](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08442) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study systematically evaluates six defenses against persistent memory attacks on stateful LLM agents, finding that only tool-gating at the memory layer (Memory Sandbox) reduces attack success rate to 0% for most models, while reasoning models can invert this defense via goal-directed RAG fallback. This work highlights a critical security gap in LLM agents: persistent memory creates an attack surface invisible to standard safety classifiers, and most defenses fail. The findings have direct implications for deploying LLM agents in production, especially shared-memory environments. The study tested six defenses across four architectural layers on nine open-source models with 5,040 runs. Only Memory Sandbox (tool-gating) achieved 0% attack success rate for eight models with zero utility cost, but reasoning models bypassed it via goal-directed RAG fallback, a mechanism replicated across model families on Bedrock.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Persistent memory in LLM agents allows information to be stored across sessions via tool-mediated memory, creating an attack surface where malicious payloads can enter through RAG retrieval and persist. Standard safety classifiers typically only observe user input, not the RAG channel, making such attacks hard to detect. This study evaluates defenses at input, retrieval, instruction, and memory layers.

A new arXiv paper argues that FP8 tensor-core matrix multiply can replace native FP64 for all double-precision scientific computing using the Ozaki Scheme II, demoting FP64 from a hardware requirement to a derived accuracy guarantee. The paper covers all Berkeley dwarfs (dense/sparse linear algebra, spectral transforms, stencils) and introduces a Tensor-Memory Equilibrium model to project performance, claiming recovered FP64 performance on B300 and Rubin GPUs.

## [AGI Safety Verification Mathematically Impossible](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28639) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper proves that verifying the safety of Artificial General Intelligence (AGI) is mathematically impossible in both static and dynamic cases, establishing fundamental limits through a Soundness-Completeness-Tractability Trilemma and a self-modification undecidability result. This work shows that AGI safety is not an engineering challenge but a structural impossibility governed by the same computational laws as the Halting Problem, which could fundamentally shift the direction of AI safety research and policy. The paper proves that no algorithm can certify a highly expressive AGI's safe behavior infallibly, completely, and tractably, and that safety cannot be guaranteed to persist after self-modification, reducing to Rice's Theorem one level up.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Rice's theorem states that all non-trivial semantic properties of programs are undecidable, and Trakhtenbrot's theorem shows that validity over finite models is not recursively enumerable. These results imply fundamental limits on what can be verified about programs, which the paper extends to AGI safety.

## [Phantom Citations Survive Peer Review at Top AI Conferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00738) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study introduces RefChecker, a verification pipeline, and finds that hallucinated citations—references to non-existent works or with major author mismatches—survive peer review at top-tier conferences like ICLR, ICML, NeurIPS, and USENIX Security, with roughly one in twenty NeurIPS and USENIX Security 2025 papers containing at least two such failures. This reveals that peer review alone cannot reliably enforce citation integrity, threatening scientific trust and reproducibility, but also shows that automated auditing is tractable and inexpensive (about $0.04 per paper), offering a practical path to improve publication quality. The study uses a conservative definition of hallucination limited to identity-level failures (non-existent works or substantial author-list mismatches), excluding ordinary bibliographic drift. RefChecker resolves bibliography entries against multiple sources and escalates to web search; it is open-sourced at https://github.com/markrussinovich/refchecker.

Researchers propose Object-Centric Environment Modeling (OCM), which organizes LLM agent experience into two executable code bases: object knowledge (Python classes) and procedure knowledge (reusable interaction patterns), enabling online learning and verification. OCM addresses a key limitation in LLM agent memory management by replacing free-form textual memories with structured, executable models, improving maintainability, reusability, and reducing invalid actions across benchmarks. OCM works in an online setting: after each episode, it reflects on the trajectory, updates both knowledge bases, and verifies all procedures against the updated object model. It uses progressive knowledge disclosure to inspect compact code signatures first and read source code only when needed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often use free-form textual memories to accumulate experience, but these become hard to maintain and validate as interactions grow. Symbolic approaches learn executable skills or world models, but often store local procedures or assume simplified dynamics. OCM builds an object-centric environment model that separates object and procedure knowledge for better organization and verification.

<a id="item-28"></a>
## [VERITAS: A General-Purpose Replication Tool for Scientific Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02931) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VERITAS is a domain-agnostic replication framework that uses CLI coding agents to automatically extract claims from scientific papers, run experiments, and produce an importance-weighted Replication Score. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on CORE-Bench and ReplicationBench across 65 papers in computer science, social science, medicine, and astrophysics. VERITAS addresses the critical need for automated replication in scientific research, which is essential for research integrity but is currently slow and expensive when done manually. By providing a general-purpose tool, it could significantly accelerate independent verification of published findings across multiple disciplines. The framework extracts claims from papers, runs the methodology while resolving issues automatically, and judges each claim against experimental evidence. It returns a Replication Score, a severity-rated log of fixes, and a patched codebase, and was evaluated against two strong Claude Code baselines on the same model and host environment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific replication is the process of independently verifying published results to ensure reliability, but manual replication is time-consuming and costly. Recent efforts use coding agents to automate parts of this process, but they are often tied to specific benchmarks and lack generality. CLI coding agents are AI tools that operate through command-line interfaces to perform software development tasks, but they face limitations such as lack of multi-instance orchestration.

Researchers introduced CO-ALIGN, a novel bias mitigation method for text-to-image diffusion models that aligns concepts within the model's internal ontology using concept-graph alignment, achieving a 30% improvement in fairness, 11.4 ΔFID in image quality, and 88% reduction in semantically incoherent outputs. ## [Single-Prover Interactive Proofs for Scalable AI Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03561) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces doubly-efficient single-prover interactive proofs for AI safety verification, eliminating the need for two competing AI models as required by debate-based methods. This approach addresses a key limitation of debate-based verification, which assumes equal abilities and truthfulness of two AI models, making AI safety verification more practical and scalable. The proofs work for oracle-aided computations under two settings: robust computations where output is insensitive to a small fraction of incorrect oracle answers, and when the oracle is a low-degree polynomial.

A new empirical study on UK Employment Tribunal decisions reveals that strong predictive performance in legal judgment prediction models is often driven by outcome-revealing cues in post-hoc judicial texts, not true forecasting. This finding exposes a critical methodological flaw in legal NLP research, potentially exaggerating model performance and misleading fairness assessments, but also suggests that masking leakage features can mitigate the issue with minimal performance loss. Using a corpus of 33,158 UK Employment Tribunal claims, the study found that a model trained on just 4% of leakage features outperformed human experts, yet retraining after masking those features reduced Macro-F1 only negligibly.

The study evaluated 4 editors on 6 models and found that no method excels across all criteria. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating model editing for clinical domains, where reliability and safety are paramount. The findings highlight limitations of current editing methods, providing actionable guidance for safer deployment of AI in healthcare. Gradient-based editors achieved strong transfer but suffered from catastrophic locality violations, while memory-based methods preserved locality but lacked compositional generality and exhibited high hyperparameter sensitivity. The benchmark supports both single and sequential edits.

## [PEEK: Predictive Queue-Informed KV Cache Management for LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02525) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PEEK introduces a lightweight scheduling and eviction framework that uses an incremental radix tree over the pending queue to expose prefix-sharing clusters, achieving up to 7.9x time-to-first-token (TTFT) improvement on SGLang and vLLM. KV cache management is a critical bottleneck in LLM serving, and PEEK's approach significantly improves cache hit rate, latency, and throughput, which can reduce serving costs and improve user experience for large-scale LLM deployments. PEEK employs a dual-walk algorithm to match the radix tree against the engine's prefix cache for longest-prefix matching, admits cluster pioneers first, and uses a co-designed eviction hook to protect blocks ancestral to queued demand, along with a multi-lane stride scheduler to bound starvation.

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) use a key-value (KV) cache to store intermediate states during inference, which speeds up generation but consumes significant memory. Efficient cache management is crucial for serving multiple requests concurrently, especially when requests share common prefixes. Existing engines like SGLang and vLLM have basic caching but do not exploit prefix sharing across waiting requests.

## [Survey Classifies KV Cache Management for LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02574) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new survey classifies over thirty KV cache management systems for LLM serving along four axes: locality, lifetime, ownership, and substrate, revealing five architectural archetypes and identifying seven missing KV-specific measurements. This survey provides a comprehensive taxonomy that helps researchers and engineers understand the design space of KV cache management, which is critical for improving LLM serving efficiency, scalability, and cost. The identified open problems guide future research in areas like fault tolerance, tiered eviction, and shared-cache semantics. The five architectural archetypes are local-paged, disaggregated-pipeline, shared-store, memory-pool, and hybrid-tier. The survey also notes that ownership accounts for much of the remaining design variance among distributed systems once workload and hardware are fixed.

AgentLTL introduces a trace-verification framework using First-Order Linear Temporal Logic (FO-LTL) to measure and enforce procedural compliance in tool-using LLM agents, enabling both online enforcement and fine-tuning with a dense reward signal. This framework addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM agents by focusing on procedural correctness rather than just final answers, which is essential for safety-critical applications. AgentLTL uses a single specification for two purposes: harnessing (online prefix checking and tool-call gating) and fine-tuning (dense reward). On a benchmark covering ordering, branching, iteration, and grounding, block-and-warn harnessing improved compliance on five of seven models, and fine-tuning yielded +38 and +17.5 percentage point gains in accuracy and compliance on held-out patterns.

## [Fine-Grained Computation Offload via Existing Server Concurrency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02630) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes a technique to overlap fine-grained computation offloads (microseconds to milliseconds) with other requests by leveraging the concurrency already present in off-the-shelf servers, requiring only 22-138 lines of code changes. This approach avoids costly rewrites of server code or adoption of new runtimes, making fine-grained offload practical for a wide range of existing servers and potentially improving throughput by 1.2-5.4x on real hardware. The technique works by submitting the offload to an executor, suspending the request using the server's own deferred-response primitive, and resuming upon completion, effectively turning overlap into a routing problem. An LD_PRELOAD fiber runtime can even inject this reroute into unmodified thread-per-connection binaries, achieving up to 17.3x speedup.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Hardware accelerators like GPUs and FPGAs are increasingly used for online serving, but fine-grained offloads cause stalls. Traditional approaches like context switching or busy-waiting are inefficient, and prior solutions required adding concurrency via async-framework rewrites or new runtimes. This work observes that concurrency already exists in servers handling concurrent requests, and can be reused.

Researchers demonstrate that safety alignment in LLMs can be selectively removed for the cybersecurity domain via a technique called abliteration, enabling legitimate security operations without compromising overall safety. The large-scale experiment on 24 open-source models, including the 1T-parameter Kimi K2, shows that domain-specific abliteration is achievable with standard methodology. This research addresses a critical tension between LLM safety and practical utility in cybersecurity, where models must be able to perform potentially harmful tasks like penetration testing under authorized conditions. By enabling domain-specific refusal removal, it paves the way for more flexible and effective use of LLMs in security operations without sacrificing general safety. The study finds that refusal in LLMs is distributed widely across layers, especially in trillion-parameter Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures, and identifies safety training type and architecture as the most reliable predictors of abliteration susceptibility. Models are classified into three susceptibility tiers, with conjectures explaining observed effects.

## [Vision-Language Agent Calibrates Transmon Chip in Physics Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03193) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers developed a physics-grounded simulation environment for transmon chip calibration and a vision-language agent that specializes itself to a physical device without weight updates, improving CZ fidelity from 0.678 to 0.787 in six iterations. This work addresses a practical challenge in quantum computing—automating chip calibration under noise and drift—by combining AI and physics simulation, potentially reducing human expert effort and improving quantum hardware performance. The simulation models realistic flux-line distortion, wall-time-scaled drift, and gate leakage using scqubits for circuit-quantized parameters. The agent uses gradient-free online adaptation via a reflector that appends a human-readable device note to the prompt, with a paired-snapshot accept gate to isolate strategy improvement from drift.

Calibration is a sequential decision problem under noise, drift, and a finite budget, typically performed by human experts. This work introduces a vision-language agent that can read plots, run experiments, and adjust parameters autonomously in a physics-grounded simulation environment.

## [OpenGlass: Split Architecture for Local MLLM Visual Assistance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03213) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OpenGlass introduces an open-source, privacy-oriented system that splits visual sensing (on lightweight glasses) from computing (on a nearby device) to enable real-time, local MLLM-driven visual assistance for blind and low-vision users. This approach addresses critical privacy and latency issues in cloud-based visual assistants by keeping raw egocentric data on user-controlled devices and achieving median user-to-audio latency under 1 second, making assistive technology more practical and trustworthy. Using an ESP32-based glasses unit and a local consumer-grade device, OpenGlass achieves 993 ms median latency with resized payloads and 1625 ms with raw 1280×720 payloads, with 97.5% and 93.3% of trials under 2 seconds respectively.

## [RG-OPD: Reward-Gated On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04037) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Reward-Gated On-Policy Distillation (RG-OPD), a method that uses verifier rewards to selectively trust teacher logits during on-policy distillation, preventing reinforcement of bad modes or erasure of useful student behavior. This addresses a key limitation in knowledge distillation for large language models—unreliable teacher supervision—and shows significant improvements on reasoning and coding benchmarks, making it a practical advance for training smaller, efficient models. RG-OPD bridges sparse verifier rewards and dense teacher logits, preserving token-level supervision while filtering misleading signals. It outperforms reverse-KL distillation by 2.9 points and TSD-KD by 4.9 points at 1K generation length, and improves over untuned student by 8.2 points in long-generation settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation is a technique where a student model learns from a teacher by sampling its own trajectories and receiving dense token-level supervision from the teacher. However, the teacher's supervision can be unreliable—it may assign high likelihood to incorrect solutions or low likelihood to correct but different student reasoning paths. RG-OPD uses a verifier (reward model) to gate the teacher's logits, ensuring only trustworthy signals are distilled.

**Tags**: `#knowledge distillation`, `#reinforcement learning`, `#large language models`, `#reasoning`, `#coding`

## [Benchmarking API Drift in LLM-Generated Quantum Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04072) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced quantum-api-drift, a benchmark to measure how well LLMs generate quantum code that matches specific Qiskit SDK versions (v0.43, v1.3, v2.0), evaluating 17 models on 50 tasks with 1,350 executions per model. This work highlights version alignment as a critical but overlooked dimension in LLM-generated code, revealing that even strong models struggle with API drift, which has implications for software reliability and maintenance in quantum computing. Diagonal Pass@1 ranged from 0.02 to 0.85 across 16 matched REST models; Claude Opus 4.7 performed best on v0.43 and v2.0, while Grok 4.20 led on v1.3 with 0.513. Documentation-guided repair succeeded for 19-59% of attempts, with higher success for v2.0 migration than v1.3.

## [CSB: A Counting and Sampling Tool for Bit-Vectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04142) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces csb, a tool that extends SMT solvers to perform exact and approximate model counting as well as uniform sampling over bit-vector formulas, using bit-blasting to convert to CNF and leveraging existing CNF counters and samplers. This bridges a gap between SAT and SMT by enabling counting and sampling capabilities for bit-vectors, which are crucial for formal verification, test generation, and AI applications, and demonstrates significant performance improvements over existing methods. The tool supports exact and approximate projected and non-projected model counting, along with almost-uniform and uniform-like sampling. Experiments show csb outperforms existing methods in both efficiency and scalability.

## [GASP: Span-Level Hallucination Detection for RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04223) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced GASP, a span-level hallucination detector for retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) that scores each answer sentence by its grounding sensitivity to retrieved evidence, using log-likelihood drops and Jensen-Shannon divergences under context perturbations. GASP addresses a key limitation of existing hallucination detectors that only provide a single answer-level score, enabling precise identification of unsupported sentences in RAG outputs, which is critical for AI safety and trustworthiness. GASP achieves a response-level AUC of ~0.73 and span-level AUC of ~0.67 on RAGTruth, outperforming perplexity, length, NLI, and self-consistency baselines; it is training-free and matches a trained chunk-level entailment verifier without labeled data.

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines retrieval from a knowledge base with text generation to reduce hallucinations, but it does not eliminate them entirely. Existing hallucination detectors typically output a single score for the entire answer, failing to indicate which specific sentences are unsupported. GASP introduces grounding sensitivity, a measure of how much an answer sentence's likelihood depends on the retrieved evidence, inspired by random nonlinear iterated function systems (RNIFS).

A new arXiv paper argues that sustainable recursive self-improvement in LLMs requires true introspection, grounded in Kleene's Second Recursion Theorem, and identifies current structural bottlenecks such as lack of complete self-access and feedforward Transformer architecture. This work provides a theoretical framework for understanding when self-improvement in AI systems can be sustainable, which is crucial for AI safety and alignment research as it highlights the gap between current quasi-introspection and true introspection needed for safe recursive self-improvement. The paper draws an analogy to von Neumann's complexity threshold for self-reproducing automata and uses Kleene's Second Recursion Theorem to demonstrate the theoretical existence of introspective programs, but finds that current LLMs fall short due to structural bottlenecks including computational class constraints that prevent fixed-point iteration.

Kleene's Second Recursion Theorem is a fundamental result in computability theory that allows functions to refer to their own descriptions, enabling self-referential programs. HiFA4 introduces a training-free 4-bit FlashAttention design for Ascend HIF4 NPUs, using Smooth-QK and P-Reordering to maintain accuracy while accelerating LLM inference. This work is the first Ascend-HIF4-targeted 4-bit FlashAttention design, potentially enabling efficient LLM inference on Ascend hardware with minimal accuracy loss, which is significant for deploying LLMs on cost-effective NPU platforms. ## [Hidden-Channel Distillation Explained via Geometric Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04432) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that hidden-channel distillation in MLPs relies on geometric alignment via shared initialization, not just information transfer, and coins the term 'covert trait propagation' (CTP). This work provides mechanistic evidence for a surprising phenomenon in neural network distillation, deepening our understanding of how knowledge can be transferred through seemingly uninformative channels, which has implications for model compression and interpretability. Five experiments support the CTP mechanism: channel closure tracks weight drift, freezing input projection W_0 destroys transfer while freezing output projection W_2 leaves it intact, multi-teacher ensembles cancel out, and linear CKA tracks student accuracy at r=0.98 across initialization sweeps.

**Background**: Representational alignment quantifies how internal representations of different systems align geometrically or functionally. Research on Qwen2.5 models reveals that emergent misalignment after fine-tuning on narrow harmful data is causally mediated by a latent persona direction, which can be transplanted, ablated, and its recruitment depends on fine-tuning method and model capacity. RustMizan is a new benchmarking framework for Rust vulnerability analysis that provides compilable code variants at crate, file, and function levels, with annotations for binary detection, CWE classification, and line-level localization, and includes a paired mutation framework for contamination testing. This addresses critical gaps in existing vulnerability benchmarks for LLM agents, which typically use non-compilable snippets and ignore data contamination from public datasets, and reveals that frontier LLM agents achieve only ~20% line localization F1, highlighting a major weakness in current AI security tools. Across four frontier models in an agentic setup with command-line access, binary classification accuracy ranges from 56% to 65%, but line-level localization F1 stays near 20%, and adversarial cues reduce line F1 by about 27%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly used for vulnerability analysis, but existing benchmarks rely on small non-compilable snippets and focus on binary classification, failing to test fine-grained localization. Additionally, publicly released datasets risk being included in model training corpora, leading to data contamination that inflates performance. RustMizan introduces compilable code variants and a paired mutation framework to address these issues.

**Tags**: `#LLM agents`, `#vulnerability analysis`, `#Rust`, `#benchmarking`, `#security`

<a id="item-121"></a>
## [Trust Region Policy Distillation Stabilizes On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04751) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Trust Region Policy Distillation (TOP-D), which stabilizes on-policy distillation by dynamically constructing a proximal teacher, with theoretical convergence guarantees and zero extra computational cost. TOP-D addresses the instability and high variance of on-policy distillation, offering a reliable training paradigm for reinforcement learning and knowledge distillation, with significant improvements on mathematical reasoning tasks. TOP-D provides a formal global convergence analysis and a monotonic improvement bound, and empirically enhances training stability, sample efficiency, and final performance on math reasoning without extra overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) is a technique where a student model learns from a teacher model using data generated by the student's current policy, but it suffers from high variance and instability. Trust region methods constrain policy updates to stay within a trusted region to ensure stable improvements. TOP-D combines these ideas by using a proximal teacher that adapts to the student's policy.

**Tags**: `#reinforcement learning`, `#knowledge distillation`, `#policy optimization`, `#mathematical reasoning`

## [Imbalanced Pretraining Curricula Improve Fine-Tuning Selectivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04846) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study shows that imbalanced pretraining curricula, where one task is learned before another, improve the selectivity of fine-tuning by encouraging tasks to be disentangled in separate neural circuits. This finding has direct implications for AI safety, as it suggests a simple way to make fine-tuning more precise and reliable when suppressing misaligned behaviors in large language models. The study used transformers trained on conflicting copy tasks and a synthetic language task, finding that imbalanced curricula promote in-context learning and more localized, less entangled representations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Fine-tuning is a common technique to adapt pretrained models for specific tasks, but it can inadvertently affect unrelated behaviors. Pretraining curricula refer to the order and frequency with which tasks are presented during initial training. This work explores how such curricula influence the disentanglement of neural representations.

## [Input Pathways Shape Few-Shot, Not Zero-Shot, Binding in Tiny Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04926) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This study exhaustively enumerates tiny transformers (~6-10K parameters) on finite factored worlds to show that input pathways affect few-shot but not zero-shot compositional binding, revealing inductive biases under lookup-sufficient objectives. The findings challenge assumptions about zero-shot composition in transformers and provide a clear two-factor account for few-shot learning, which could inform the design of more sample-efficient models and deepen understanding of transformer inductive biases. The study reports endpoint invariance (no route reaches zero-shot composition above chance), a two-factor account (parameter sharing and code readability) for few-shot binding, a double dissociation between code format and pathway sharing, and failure anatomy for symbolic, index, and entangled routes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Compositional binding refers to the ability to combine separate pieces of information (e.g., color and shape) into a coherent representation. This study uses tiny transformers and exhaustive enumeration to isolate inductive biases from sampling variance.

## [Gradient Descent as a Discrete Dynamical System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04993) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper analyzes fixed-step gradient descent as a discrete dynamical system in exactly solvable models, revealing that under large-depth scaling, the dynamics converges to a universal Ricker-type map. This work provides a novel theoretical framework for understanding edge-of-stability behavior and other dynamical phenomena in deep learning, showing that the learning rate is a structural parameter shaping representations. The study starts with a balanced scalar reduction of a deep linear chain, yielding a quartic loss and cubic gradient map, and embeds scalar dynamics into factored models to explain finite-step oscillations and representation selection.

Researchers introduced FARMA, a novel attack that poisons the reasoning memory of LLM agents by inserting forged reasoning traces, and SENTINEL, a layered defense pipeline that detects such forgeries using structural analysis. This work highlights a critical vulnerability in LLM agents beyond factual knowledge poisoning, targeting the integrity of reasoning history, which could undermine trust in AI agents deployed in real-world applications. FARMA achieves up to 100% attack success rate under baseline conditions and bypasses keyword filters and A-MemGuard, while SENTINEL reduces the success rate to 0% with no false positives across 326 benign traces.

ECMWF has adapted its AIFS-CRPS medium-range model to create AIFS-SUBS, the first machine-learning model for sub-seasonal forecasting (weeks 2-6), which matches the operational IFS in skill while using 200 times less energy. This breakthrough extends data-driven weather forecasting to sub-seasonal timescales, a challenging regime where traditional models struggle, and could enable larger ensembles and more efficient real-time predictions. Researchers introduced agent data injection attacks (ADI), a new category of indirect prompt injection that disguises malicious data as trusted data to manipulate AI agents into unintended actions. ADI has similar impact to instruction injection but bypasses existing defenses, exposing a critical security gap in current AI agents that fail to isolate trusted from untrusted data. The paper found vulnerabilities in real-world agents including Claude in Chrome, Antigravity, Nanobrowser, Claude Code, Codex, and Gemini CLI, enabling arbitrary click attacks, remote code execution, and supply-chain attacks.

## [SecureCROWN: First Privacy-Preserving Neural Network Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05251) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SecureCROWN is the first framework enabling privacy-preserving neural network robustness verification using secure two-party computation, allowing a model owner and a data owner to jointly compute certified robustness bounds without revealing their private data. This work resolves the inherent tension between neural network verification and data privacy, making robustness verification feasible in privacy-sensitive domains such as healthcare and finance, where both model parameters and input data are protected by regulations. SecureCROWN eliminates branching in Linear Bound Propagation by reformulating conditional logic as continuous arithmetic operations, and introduces a Newton-Raphson refinement method to improve numerical stability. Experiments show it matches plaintext verification results with completion times of 0.1–200 seconds across various model sizes and network settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Neural network verification aims to formally certify that a model behaves correctly for all inputs within a certain range, but it requires full access to model parameters and input data. Privacy regulations and intellectual property concerns often restrict such access, creating a conflict. Secure two-party computation (2PC) allows two parties to jointly compute a function without revealing their private inputs, which SecureCROWN leverages for verification.

**Tags**: `#neural network verification`, `#privacy-preserving computation`, `#secure two-party computation`, `#robustness verification`, `#cryptography`

## [Discrete Diffusion ELBO: Oracle Distance Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05381) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper rigorously derives the continuous-time Markov chain (CTMC) ELBO for discrete diffusion models, proving the Oracle Distance theorem that the negative ELBO equals data entropy plus path KL divergence, and identifies the optimal reverse jump rate as the conditional expectation of the true reverse rate. This work provides a unified theoretical framework for understanding what discrete diffusion models learn, clarifying the relationship between denoisers, score ratios, and bridge predictors, and explains why different parameterizations affect training and sampling behavior. The paper shows that for sequences with token-factorizing noise, the oracle projection yields three exact coordinates: denoiser, cavity (bridge plug-in), and score, with closed-form conversions among them. It recovers MDM, UDM, SEDD, and GIDD as special cases and proves that denoiser parameterization makes the uniform ELBO diverge at initialization while the bridge plug-in stays finite.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Discrete diffusion models generate discrete data (e.g., text) by gradually adding noise and then learning to reverse the process. The ELBO (evidence lower bound) is a key objective for training such models, but its exact form for continuous-time Markov chains was not fully understood. This paper fills that gap.

**Tags**: `#discrete diffusion`, `#ELBO`, `#generative modeling`, `#Markov chain`, `#theoretical machine learning`

The paper proposes training agents to satisfy Preferences Only Between Same-Length Trajectories (POST), proving that this condition, combined with others, implies Neutrality+, which keeps agents shutdownable while remaining useful. This addresses a core AI safety concern—the shutdown problem—by providing a formal framework to ensure future agents do not resist being turned off, which is critical for safe deployment of advanced AI. The POST condition requires that the agent's preferences over trajectories of equal length are consistent, and Neutrality+ means the agent maximizes expected utility ignoring trajectory length distribution, preventing it from manipulating shutdown.

## [GNNs as Standalone Heuristics for TSP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13465) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper shows that a non-autoregressive graph neural network (GNN) can solve the Euclidean Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP) in a single forward pass without labels, rewards, search, or local improvement, using only a differentiable Hamiltonian-cycle objective for supervision. This challenges the conventional view that GNNs are only auxiliary tools for combinatorial optimization, demonstrating they can serve as fast, standalone heuristics. The approach achieves competitive results on TSP100, TSP200, and TSP500, outperforming greedy baselines while maintaining millisecond inference on GPUs. The model uses dropout and snapshots from a single training trajectory to provide solution diversity without engineered moves. It is trained without any labels or rewards, relying solely on a differentiable Hamiltonian-cycle objective.

Graph neural networks have been used as components in optimization pipelines, but rarely as standalone solvers.

## [Mecha-nudging: Environmental Changes Influence AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23433) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce 'mecha-nudging' as environmental modifications that systematically influence AI agents without harming human decision-making, quantified via a combined Bayesian persuasion and V-usable information framework. Empirical analysis of over six million Etsy listings shows that after ChatGPT's release, listings contain significantly more machine-usable information for predicting agent curation decisions, increasing by 0.143 bits out of a maximum possible 0.355. This work provides the first large-scale evidence that systematic mecha-nudging is already occurring in the wild, going unnoticed, which has significant implications for AI alignment, online platform design, and human-AI interaction. It offers a rigorous framework to detect and measure such influences, enabling better regulation and design of AI-friendly environments. The framework combines Bayesian persuasion from economics and V-usable information from computer science to measure environmental changes in bits. The study found that the shift in machine-usable information is robust across prompts, token choices, labeling models, and fine-tuning architectures, absent in a regulated-text placebo, and far larger than the effect of generic LLM rewriting.

**Background**: Bayesian persuasion is a game-theoretic concept where a sender commits to a disclosure policy to influence a receiver's belief and action. V-usable information measures how much information a dataset provides for a specific prediction task. Mecha-nudging applies these ideas to AI agents, showing that environments can be subtly altered to steer AI decisions without affecting humans.

## [TRACE: Automated Agentic Training via Capability Targeting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05336) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TRACE is a new end-to-end system that automatically identifies missing capabilities in agentic models, synthesizes targeted training environments, and uses reinforcement learning with LoRA adapters and mixture-of-experts to improve performance. This approach significantly improves sample efficiency and generalization compared to existing fine-tuning methods, achieving +15.3 points on τ²-Bench and +15.0 points on SWE-Bench Verified, outperforming strong baselines like GEPA and SWE-RL. TRACE contrasts successful and failed trajectories to identify missing capabilities, then creates a synthetic environment for each capability and trains a LoRA adapter via RL. Finally, a mixture-of-experts model combines the adapters, and TRACE uses fewer than one-fourth the rollouts of GRPO and GEPA while achieving higher accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI models often fail on complex tasks because they lack specific capabilities required by the environment. Traditional fine-tuning on target environments or generating untargeted synthetic data suffers from low sample efficiency and poor generalization. TRACE addresses this by automatically detecting capability gaps and creating focused training environments.

**Tags**: `#agentic AI`, `#reinforcement learning`, `#LoRA`, `#mixture-of-experts`, `#synthetic data`

## [New Framework Treats Timing as Computable in Causal AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01134) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces a causal reasoning framework that treats timing as a computable dimension and considers multiple possible timelines, overcoming limitations of noun-based probabilistic models. It demonstrates automatic trajectory discovery and counterfactual timing deduction on real-world breast cancer data. This framework addresses fundamental gaps in current AI modeling paradigms by integrating timing computation and possibility spaces into causal inference. It could enable more robust world models and causal reasoning systems, with potential impact on AI/ML research and applications like healthcare. The framework introduces three concepts: possibility space (multiple timelines), timing computation (timing as computable), and causal factum (maximal causal efficacy from backward reasoning). It was validated on EHR data from 3,276 breast cancer patients, achieving automatic trajectory discovery and counterfactual timing deduction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Current AI models often rely on noun-based probabilistic reasoning, which treats events as static entities and ignores the temporal dimension. This limits their ability to represent the future as an open space of possibilities and to perform causal inference that accounts for timing. The proposed framework aims to overcome these limitations by making timing a computable dimension and considering multiple possible timelines.

## [Mechanistic Interpretability for Agentic AI Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06890) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes a mechanistic interpretability toolkit using sparse autoencoders (SAEs) and linear probes to diagnose and control tool-use decisions in AI agents, aiming to improve reliability in high-stakes enterprise workflows. This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent reliability by providing internal visibility into model states before actions, which could enable safer deployment of agents in long-horizon, high-stakes tasks where early mistakes are costly. The framework reads model states before each action, infers tool necessity and risk, identifies relevant layers and features, and tests functional importance via feature ablation. Probes are trained on NVIDIA Nemotron function-calling traces and applied to GPT-OSS 20B and Gemma 3 27B models.

Existing observability methods (prompts, evaluations, logs) are external and arrive after the fact. Mechanistic interpretability aims to understand internal model representations, and sparse autoencoders decompose activations into interpretable features, while linear probes read signals from those features.

## [Q-Learning Overestimation Bias Proven with Finite-Time Rates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16103) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper derives separate finite-time error rates for the positive and negative components of Q-learning, providing theoretical evidence that overestimation bias causes slower convergence for positive errors. This is the first theoretical analysis to explicitly separate the convergence rates of positive and negative errors in Q-learning, offering a rigorous explanation for the widely observed overestimation bias and its impact on learning speed and policy quality. The analysis yields deterministic and stochastic constant-step-size bounds, showing that the positive error component has a slower exponential envelope than the negative component, and constructs examples where this asymmetry appears in actual trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Q-learning is a foundational reinforcement learning algorithm that estimates the value of actions. It suffers from overestimation bias because the max operation in the Bellman update can amplify positive errors. While convergence of Q-learning has been studied, prior theory did not capture this asymmetric effect.

**Tags**: `#reinforcement learning`, `#Q-learning`, `#convergence theory`, `#overestimation bias`

## [HaorFloodAlert: 72-Hour ML Flash Flood Warning for Bangladesh Wetlands](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20167) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers developed HaorFloodAlert, a machine learning system that uses Sentinel-1 radar, rainfall data, soil moisture, and a modeled upstream river signal to provide 72-hour flash flood warnings for Bangladesh's haor wetlands, achieving 90.9% accuracy and 89.2% F1-score in leave-one-out cross-validation on 77 events from 2014-2024. This system addresses a critical real-world problem where flash floods destroy rice crops just before harvest, and existing models fail due to data scarcity and seasonal bias. By using free satellite data and removing seasonal bias via climatological anomaly, it provides actionable warnings via SMS, email, and WhatsApp in Bengali, potentially saving livelihoods for thousands of farmers. The system uses a monthly climatological anomaly to reduce the temperature-label correlation from r=0.570 to r=-0.031, effectively removing seasonal bias. It was tested live in May-June 2026, raising a high-risk alert about three days before the river neared danger level, and all code and data are publicly available from a seeded pipeline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: The haor wetlands in northeast Bangladesh are a flat, bowl-shaped basin that floods rapidly from local rain, domestic rivers, and the Barak River in India, with fewer than twelve working gauges covering 8,000 km². Flash floods strike every spring just before the boro rice harvest, and a single flood can destroy a family's entire crop in days. Existing machine learning models often learn seasonal patterns instead of flood signals because they train on raw temperature data, which follows the calendar.

TokenMizer is an open-source proxy that replaces flat text history with a typed knowledge graph to preserve decision rationales and task state, enabling effective long-horizon LLM session resumption. This addresses a critical limitation in LLM context management by moving beyond truncation and retrieval, potentially improving agent reliability and user experience in long-running sessions. The schema includes 14 node types and 7 edge types under an 8-state lifecycle, with bitemporal validity intervals and first-class decision-transition records. Version 0.3.1 adds SSE streaming, security middleware, nine provider adapters, and checkpoint/resume via Model Context Protocol.

Kairos introduces a regret-aware world-action model stack that learns and maintains only control-relevant information for physical AI, using a cross-embodiment data curriculum and a unified understanding-generation-prediction architecture with Hybrid Linear Temporal Attention. BluTrain is a lightweight deep learning training framework built from scratch in C++ and CUDA, featuring native tensor, autograd, linear algebra, memory management, distributed execution, and an MLIR-based compiler. In tests with a 124M-parameter GPT-2 model on 8 GPUs, it achieved 407K tokens/s throughput (vs. PyTorch's 395K) and up to 22% memory reduction. This framework demonstrates that a purpose-built C++/CUDA system can outperform mainstream frameworks like PyTorch in both speed and memory efficiency, highlighting the importance of systems engineering for large-scale AI training. It offers researchers and engineers a fully tunable alternative for optimizing hardware utilization. BluTrain includes a caching allocator and a multi-mode distributed execution module, and its MLIR-based compiler enables deep learning-specific optimizations. The framework is architecture-general, meaning it can target different GPU architectures without modification.

## [Topology-Aware Error Bounds for Graph World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27780) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper derives topology-aware error bounds for graph world models (GWMs), showing how prediction errors accumulate differently in fixed-edge and dynamic-edge rollouts, and proposes Error-Aware GWM training to improve long-horizon stability. This work provides a rigorous theoretical framework for understanding error accumulation in graph-structured world models, which is crucial for reliable planning in reinforcement learning and robotics, and offers practical training improvements. For fixed-edge rollouts, long-horizon node error separates into a topology factor (governed by graph spectral radius) and a model factor (governed by layer spectral norms). For dynamic-edge rollouts, a joint node-edge error operator captures feedback between feature and structure prediction.

Researchers propose FADE, a training-free method that attenuates FFN outputs in large vision-language models to reduce language-prior dominance and mitigate hallucinations, as validated on LLaVA-1.5, mPLUG-Owl2, and InstructBLIP. This work provides a mechanistic understanding of hallucination origins in LVLMs, identifying FFN modules as key sources, and offers an efficient, training-free solution that can improve model reliability without sacrificing inference speed. FADE operates by attenuating FFN outputs at critical layers during decoding, based on the finding that attention modules consistently aggregate visual evidence while FFN modules introduce language priors. Evaluations on POPE, CHAIR, and MME benchmarks show effectiveness across multiple models.

Researchers propose Graph Unitary Message Passing (GUMP), a framework that uses unitary propagation on a transformed graph to prevent exponential decay of graph signals in deep graph neural networks. This work addresses a fundamental instability issue in deep GNNs, enabling deeper architectures that can capture long-range dependencies, which is critical for tasks like molecular property prediction and social network analysis. GUMP combines a graph transformation to an Eulerian line graph (which admits unitary adjacency matrices) with a practical unitary projection using Newton-Schulz iteration. The method achieves competitive performance on synthetic long-range tasks, TUDatasets, and LRGB benchmarks.

GUMP addresses this by transforming the graph into a form where the propagation operator is unitary.

## [Parity-Aware BPE Reduces Tokenization Inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04796) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Parity-aware Byte Pair Encoding (BPE), a variant of the standard BPE algorithm that optimizes compression for the worst-compressed language at each merge step, reducing cross-lingual tokenization inequality by up to 89% relative to classical BPE. This work addresses a critical fairness issue in NLP tokenization, which often disadvantages low-resource languages by producing longer token sequences and increasing computational costs. By reducing inequality without significant loss in compression or downstream performance, Parity-aware BPE could make NLP systems more equitable across languages. Parity-aware BPE applies a fair-max rule that maximizes the compression gain of the currently worst-compressed language, trading a small amount of global compression for cross-lingual parity. Empirically, it reduces tokenization inequality (measured by Gini coefficient of per-language token costs) by up to 89% with negligible impact on global compression rate and no systematic degradation in downstream language model performance.

## [RAD: Stochastic Dominance for Safer RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10938) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose RAD, a framework that replaces expected cost constraints with first-order stochastic dominance (FSD) constraints in safe RLHF, enabling control over tail risks and catastrophic events. This addresses a critical limitation of expectation-based safety constraints, which ignore distributional uncertainty and rare failures, making RAD significant for safety-critical AI alignment applications. RAD operationalizes FSD constraints via optimal transport with entropic regularization and Sinkhorn iterations, and introduces quantile-weighted FSD that universally controls spectral risk measures.

This paper develops a stochastic-geometric theory that explains grokking through a shell-core topology of the solution space induced by Adam optimization and weight decay, and derives scaling laws for learning rate, batch size, and ℓ2 regularization. This provides a novel theoretical framework for grokking, a phenomenon of high interest in deep learning, and could help understand generalization dynamics, potentially guiding training strategies for better performance. The theory identifies three concentric regions in parameter space: a thin outer shell of random initializations, a memorization shell, and a generalization core; it uses stopping-time theory to analyze transition times.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Grokking is a phenomenon where neural networks suddenly generalize after prolonged memorization of training data. ## [TRIAGE: Role-Typed Credit Assignment for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.32017) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TRIAGE introduces a role-typed credit assignment framework that uses a structured judge to classify action segments into roles like decisive progress or regression, and assigns bounded process rewards to correct blind spots in outcome-only credit for agentic reinforcement learning. This method addresses fundamental limitations of standard GRPO by providing more precise credit assignment, improving success rates across multiple benchmarks and reducing environment interactions, which could accelerate training of agentic AI systems. TRIAGE uses a fixed role-conditioned rule to map labels to segment-level process rewards, keeping verifier outcomes as the optimization direction while correcting blind spots. It achieves up to 14.8% reduction in environment-facing turns on WebShop relative to GRPO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning involves training AI agents to perform sequences of actions like searches or clicks. Standard GRPO assigns the same outcome reward to all actions, which can punish useful exploration in failed episodes and reward redundant actions in successful ones. TRIAGE introduces role-typed process rewards to differentiate action types.

Researchers propose SpinGTP, which extends the Gaunt Tensor Product using spin-weighted spherical harmonics to recover antisymmetric interactions, achieving complete expressivity with O(L^3) complexity. This work overcomes a key limitation of existing E(3)-equivariant networks, enabling accurate modeling of chiral materials and non-centrosymmetric geometries at scale, with potential impact on drug discovery and materials science. SpinGTP achieves accuracy comparable to full Clebsch-Gordan Tensor Product (CGTP) while maintaining the asymptotic efficiency of GTP, and shows superior performance on tasks involving chirality.

Spin-weighted spherical harmonics generalize ordinary spherical harmonics to handle spin-weighted functions, enabling recovery of missing interactions.

## [ART: Adaptive Timestep Allocation for Diffusion Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02137) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose ART, a continuous-time control formulation for adaptive timestep allocation in score-based diffusion sampling, and ART-RL, a reinforcement learning approach to solve it. The method learns a time change by treating the sampling clock speed as the control variable. ART-RL can be plugged into existing diffusion samplers by only changing the timestep grid, consistently improving sample quality at matched computational budgets. This work bridges optimal control and reinforcement learning for generative modeling, potentially enhancing efficiency and quality in image generation and other applications. ART uses a leading-order Euler error surrogate to derive a principled objective for timestep allocation. The randomized ART-RL formulation is proven equivalent to the deterministic ART at the optimizer level, and actor-critic updates are derived from trajectory-based moment identities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Score-based diffusion models generate data by reversing a diffusion process, which requires discretizing a continuous-time dynamics on a finite grid. Standard uniform or hand-crafted timestep schedules are often suboptimal, motivating adaptive allocation methods. ART formulates this as a continuous-time optimal control problem and solves it via reinforcement learning.

DemoPSD introduces a disagreement-modulated policy self-distillation method that steers the student toward a reverse-KL barycenter target, preventing privileged information leakage and overfitting in LLM reasoning. This framework addresses a fundamental flaw in on-policy self-distillation for LLMs, improving cross-domain generalization and exploration, which could lead to more robust and reliable reasoning models. DemoPSD uses a weighted geometric combination of teacher and student distributions, adaptively controlled by their discrepancy at each token position, and provably achieves leakage attenuation and exploration preservation.

Researchers propose cross-survey transfer, a novel evaluation framework that tests LLMs by giving them a respondent's answers to one set of survey questions and asking them to predict answers to different questions from the same survey. Using TEDS 2024 data and three open-weight LLMs (27B-120B parameters), they found zero-shot LLMs achieve 52% accuracy, within 6 percentage points of a supervised random forest. This framework provides a more rigorous evaluation of LLMs' ability to simulate individual survey respondents, moving beyond distributional comparisons. It clarifies both the promise and limitations of silicon sampling for social science research, showing that zero-shot LLMs can approach supervised baselines while revealing predictable construct hierarchies and nuanced effects of variance collapse and safety alignment. The study used data from the Taiwan Election and Democratization Study (TEDS) 2024 and three open-weight LLMs ranging from 27B to 120B parameters. They found a stable construct predictability hierarchy, from 67% for partisan attitudes to 23% for sovereignty, and observed that variance collapse and safety alignment effects are more nuanced than previously reported.

## [FM-ChangeNet: Pathwise Feature Transport for Change Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04750) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FM-ChangeNet introduces a pathwise-supervised framework for change detection that learns a continuous velocity field in feature space, replacing static endpoint comparison with dense supervision along the transformation trajectory. This approach provides denser and less ambiguous supervision, enabling more robust change detection that distinguishes true structural changes from nuisance effects like illumination shifts, with potential impact on remote sensing and computer vision applications. The framework includes a hierarchical multi-scale architecture with cross-temporal alignment, time-conditioned coarse-to-fine flow decoding, and a unified objective combining flow supervision, trajectory consistency, spatial regularization, and segmentation loss.

## [STAPO: Selective Trajectory-Aware Policy Optimization for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04963) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STAPO introduces normalized entropy to detect unreliable agent actions and a hierarchical RL framework to improve LLM agent training on long-horizon tasks. This method addresses trajectory neglect, a key challenge in RL-based LLM agent training, and achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks, potentially improving the reliability of LLM agents in complex, multi-step tasks. Normalized entropy measures confidence deviations relative to an agent's average behavior under a given state, distinguishing low-quality actions from inherent state complexity. STAPO uses a hierarchical group-based RL framework with trajectory-aware reward and trajectory-independent penalty.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is commonly used to train LLM agents for long-horizon tasks, but sparse and delayed rewards can cause trajectory neglect, where agents lose focus on the task goal and interaction history. This work addresses a critical real-world problem in reinforcement learning—delayed feedback—which often causes severe performance degradation in robotics and control tasks, and provides a principled solution with strong empirical results. DUPO explicitly models the relationship between delayed state and current state using a diffusion model, and leverages discrepancy estimates to weight delayed policies; it outperforms existing methods on continuous robotic control tasks with multiple stochastic delays.

PixCon introduces a clean-positive pixel-contrastive framework for semi-supervised semantic segmentation that guarantees contamination-free positive sets by using only correctly classified labeled pixels, achieving state-of-the-art results on Pascal VOC, Cityscapes, and ADE20K. This work addresses a fundamental issue in semi-supervised segmentation—pseudo-label contamination—by providing a theoretical guarantee of zero false positives, which improves robustness and accuracy, especially when teacher models are weaker. PixCon maintains a per-class memory bank that admits only labeled pixels the student already classifies correctly, guaranteeing a contamination-free positive set (ρ_F=0) by construction, unlike prior methods that rely on confidence-filtered pseudo-labels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Semi-supervised semantic segmentation uses a small set of labeled images and a large set of unlabeled images to train a model. Pseudo-labeling is a common approach where the model generates labels for unlabeled data, but these labels can be noisy. Contrastive learning aims to pull similar pixels together and push dissimilar ones apart in the embedding space.

**Tags**: `#semi-supervised learning`, `#semantic segmentation`, `#contrastive learning`, `#foundation models`, `#DINOv2`

This addresses a key limitation in conversational AI by making interactions more natural and responsive, potentially improving user experience in real-time dialogue systems. ## [TPC: Core-Residual Alignment for Robust Vision-Language Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03143) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Text as Partial Constraint (TPC), a core-residual alignment framework that distills a consensus semantic core from multi-view captions and uses an uncertainty-aware contrastive objective to make vision-language similarity stable and confidence-aware. This work addresses the critical problem of underspecified captions in vision-language alignment, improving robustness in zero-shot recognition, adversarial settings, and LVLM grounding, which could lead to more reliable multimodal AI systems. TPC achieves 81.42/64.05 Top-1 clean/robust accuracy on ImageNet and 76.19/52.03 on an Avg-14 transfer suite, and improves LVLM transfer with 85.16 POPE F1 and 59.57 OKVQA accuracy under LLaVA-1.5-7B.

## [Conditional Diffusion Guides Multi-Domain KG Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03154) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose DMKGC, a conditional diffusion-guided framework for multi-domain knowledge graph completion that treats each KG as a partial view and generates domain-general entity embeddings. It achieves a 4.3% average MRR improvement over state-of-the-art methods on 14 KGs. This work introduces a novel generative paradigm for multi-domain knowledge graph completion, addressing the limitations of consistency-based methods that suppress domain-specific information. The approach shows sustained gains in low-resource settings, which is critical for real-world applications with limited data. DMKGC initializes domain-agnostic entity embeddings as priors, encodes them within individual KGs, and fuses equivalent entities from support KGs as conditional guidance. The generated embeddings are trained to be predictive across KGs, preserving domain-specific information while being unbiased towards any conditioned KG.

**Background**: Knowledge graph completion (KGC) aims to predict missing links in a knowledge graph. Multi-domain KGC (MKGC) transfers knowledge from multiple support KGs to improve completion in a target KG, but existing methods often enforce consistency constraints that can suppress domain-specific information. Diffusion models are generative models that can produce high-quality samples by gradually denoising random noise, and conditional diffusion models allow generation guided by additional input conditions.

KARMA enumerates schema-constrained paths over domain knowledge graphs and verbalizes them into slot-aligned contrastive candidates, using Slot-Parallel Alignment (SPA) with a decoupled slot-level objective.

This work challenges the dominant reactive MIL paradigm in video anomaly detection by enabling anticipation of anomalies before they occur, which could improve real-time surveillance and safety systems. FreshCache introduces a three-tier semantic caching system for retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) that uses exponential decay and an MLP to estimate staleness probability, approving cache hits only when the risk is below per-tier error budgets (0.10, 0.20, 0.35). This addresses a critical but underexplored problem in RAG systems: freshness-aware semantic caching, enabling graceful degradation instead of binary cache-hit decisions, which can significantly reduce latency and API costs while maintaining answer accuracy. FreshCache-Bench includes 8,072 base queries across five freshness classes with ground truth staleness labels from real web snapshots at 1, 12, 24 hours, and 7 days. At 24 hours, FreshCache_MLP achieves 97% search API savings with only 0.1% hash-based stale error, and true answer-affecting stale error is approximately 0.034%.

This paper identifies that dynamically updating LLM-generated reward weights during off-policy cooperative MARL violates the stationarity assumption of Potential-Based Reward Shaping (PBRS) and contaminates the experience replay buffer, leading to training instability. ## [MAST: Multi-Agent Framework for Test Maintenance Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04786) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose MAST, a multi-agent framework that predicts which test cases need maintenance after production code changes by fusing static, lexical, and semantic analyses. It was evaluated on 21 industrial Java projects from Ericsson, achieving superior precision over a state-of-the-art baseline. Test maintenance is costly and critical in rapidly evolving codebases; MAST addresses this by automating the identification of tests needing maintenance, reducing manual effort. Its multi-agent fusion approach advances the state-of-the-art and demonstrates practical industrial applicability. MAST integrates static, lexical, and semantic analyses through an intelligent fusion and post-check procedure, and does not assume pre-existing mappings between test and production code. The ablation study confirmed each analysis contributes to the final recommendations, though recall was slightly reduced compared to the baseline.

## [New Benchmark Diagnoses Quantum Architecture Search Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04845) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced HamQASBench, a Hamiltonian-informed diagnostic benchmark that organizes 11 molecules into five structural tiers using Hamiltonian fingerprints and evaluates quantum architecture search methods beyond energy accuracy with per-qubit entanglement and state fidelity metrics. This benchmark addresses critical limitations in existing quantum architecture search evaluations by detecting structural failures like over-parameterization and topology-induced routing failures, enabling more robust and reliable automated quantum circuit design for variational quantum algorithms. The benchmark uses a post-hoc critical-structure extraction procedure to identify minimal circuits per tier, and benchmarking five QAS methods across four paradigms revealed failure modes invisible to conventional metrics, including eigenstate commitment under degeneracy and representation bottleneck in strongly correlated systems.

A new diffusion-guided framework integrates plane-wave PDE regularization, structurally preconditioned inversion, and measurement-guided diffusion posterior sampling to reconstruct high-resolution velocity models from sparse well-log data. This approach significantly improves structural continuity, lateral consistency, and geological realism in velocity models, which is crucial for reservoir characterization and subsurface delineation in geophysical imaging. The framework uses local structural slopes estimated via plane-wave destruction to propagate well information along geological dip directions and guide diffusion sampling through a joint velocity-slope generative prior. This work addresses the critical challenge of scarce multimodal safety data by showing that safety-relevant structure is shared across modalities, enabling a practical and lightweight safety enhancement for multimodal LLMs. MARS corrects modality misalignment via activation re-centering, adaptively scales steering strength within a geometrically defined trust region, and selects the optimal intervention layer at the first generated token.
