## [PedNStream: Open-Source Pedestrian Network Flow Simulator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01021) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PedNStream is a new open-source Python simulator for macroscopic pedestrian network flow that extends the Link Transmission Model with stochastic dynamics and utility-based route choice, and provides modular controller interfaces for interventions like gating and route guidance. This fills a gap in open-source tools for large-scale pedestrian simulation that are both computationally efficient and compatible with feedback-based control, enabling researchers and practitioners to test crowd management strategies in a realistic, scalable testbed. The simulator incorporates stochastic link dynamics to capture diffusion and activity-induced variability, and replaces dynamic user equilibrium with a utility-based route choice model suited for uncertain, intervention-driven settings. It was evaluated on synthetic scenarios, real-network experiments, and a closed-loop case study demonstrating controller integration.

## [IRFE-ECG: Continual ECG Deployment Without Raw Replay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01674) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces IRFE-ECG, a method for continual ECG deployment that freezes a pretrained backbone and builds an incremental expert bank, enabling new data sources to be incorporated without replaying raw ECGs. This addresses a practical bottleneck in real-world ECG monitoring where raw data cannot be retained due to privacy or storage constraints, enabling models to adapt to new sources while preserving performance on old ones. IRFE-ECG uses frozen 1024-dimensional ECGFounder features, adds a balanced-softmax linear expert per domain, and employs a validation-calibrated margin rule to fuse the top-2 experts, achieving 0.7782 Macro-F1 without source IDs, close to the oracle 0.7915.

IRFE-ECG separates expert retention (keeping knowledge for each source) from source inference (identifying which source a sample belongs to), using frozen features and a lightweight router.

Researchers propose Ensemble Elastic DQN (EEDQN), which combines adaptive elastic multi-step returns with horizon-dependent ensemble aggregation to reduce overestimation bias in deep value-based reinforcement learning. The paper introduces Last Layer Hamiltonian Monte Carlo (LL-HMC), which restricts HMC sampling to the final layer of a deep neural network, significantly reducing computational cost while maintaining competitive uncertainty estimation performance. Last layer approaches restrict probabilistic methods to the final layer of a neural network, reducing complexity while retaining uncertainty estimates.

This paper introduces FMCT and EFMCT, frameworks that apply Flow Matching (FM) to sparse-view CT reconstruction, using deterministic ODE-based sampling instead of stochastic diffusion models. This work addresses two key limitations of diffusion-based CT reconstruction: stochastic noise interfering with data consistency corrections and high computational cost. By enabling deterministic, efficient sampling, Flow Matching could make generative-model-based CT reconstruction more practical for time-critical clinical settings. The authors provide theoretical analysis showing that the error from velocity reuse is bounded when combined with data consistency operations. Experiments demonstrate that FMCT/EFMCT achieve competitive reconstruction quality while significantly improving computational efficiency compared to diffusion-based methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse-view CT reconstruction aims to produce high-quality images from fewer X-ray projections, reducing radiation dose but making the problem ill-posed. Generative models like diffusion models have been used as priors, but they rely on stochastic differential equations (SDEs) that introduce noise and require many sampling steps. Flow Matching is an alternative generative framework that models the probability flow as a deterministic ODE, offering smoother trajectories and faster sampling.

## [IEC: Error Counting as Reward for Reference-Free RL Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05659) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Implicit Error Counting (IEC), a reward mechanism for reinforcement learning post-training that enumerates severity-weighted errors instead of checking correctness against a rubric, designed for tasks without ideal reference answers. IEC fills a gap in current RLVR and RaR methods by handling reference-free settings where multiple valid outputs exist, potentially improving AI alignment in real-world tasks like virtual try-on that lack a single correct answer. The method requires two design choices for stability: implicit score emission and group calibration. It is validated on virtual try-on (VTO) using a new benchmark MDressBench and metric CEC, outperforming RaR on all metrics.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) and Rubrics as Rewards (RaR) rely on clear correctness signals or ideal reference answers to generate evaluation criteria. IEC addresses this by counting what the response gets wrong instead of what it gets right.

OrthoReg introduces orthogonal regularization to prevent neural components from redundantly learning symbolic parts in hybrid symbolic-neural dynamical system models. ## [Learning Cardiac Motion Priors for Implicit Neural Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00955) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper compares four strategies for learning cardiac motion priors in implicit neural representations, including population prior, consensus prior, auto-decoders, and meta-learning, showing improved adaptation over random initialization. This work addresses the under-explored area of learning priors for cardiac motion INRs, which can significantly improve tracking accuracy and adaptation speed in medical imaging, potentially benefiting clinical diagnosis and treatment planning. Using short-axis tagged cardiac magnetic resonance images from the UK Biobank, the study found that all learned priors substantially improved early adaptation performance, with auto-decoders recovering large deformations faster and meta-learning maintaining the best adaptation trajectory over 50 iterations.

**Background**: Implicit neural representations (INRs) are neural networks that map continuous coordinates to continuous outputs, enabling compact and differentiable representations of motion fields. ## [TurnNat: Automatic Evaluation of Turn-Taking Naturalness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01345) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TurnNat, a likelihood-based framework that automatically evaluates turn-taking naturalness in dyadic spoken dialogue using a causal prediction model and turn-taking boundary units. This addresses a critical gap in automatic evaluation of turn-taking naturalness, which is essential for improving full-duplex spoken dialogue systems and enabling objective comparison of heterogeneous timing failures. TurnNat computes negative log-likelihood of observed voice activity over turn-taking boundary units, then aggregates mean and tail scores into a dialogue-level naturalness score; it also introduces a controlled perturbation benchmark validated by human judgments.

**Background**: Multimodal recommendation systems use item-side modality information (e.g., images, text) to improve recommendation quality. Existing methods often use raw modality features to build item-item graphs, which can introduce noise, and apply uniform alignment weights that limit effectiveness.

<a id="item-407"></a>
## [2D Projections Approximate 3D Segmentation for Spine Fracture Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15235) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a pipeline that uses 2D projections to approximate 3D vertebra segmentation for cervical spine fracture detection, achieving performance comparable to full 3D segmentation. This approach reduces computational complexity by shifting segmentation to a lower-dimensional domain, potentially enabling faster and more efficient automated fracture diagnosis in clinical settings. The pipeline uses YOLOv8 for spine localization, DenseNet121-Unet for 2D mask segmentation from sagittal and coronal projections, and an ensemble of 2.5D CNN-Transformer models for fracture classification, achieving patient-level F1 of 82.26 and AUC of 90.95.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Cervical spine fractures require rapid diagnosis from CT scans, but full 3D segmentation is computationally expensive. YOLOv8 is a real-time object detection system that requires only one forward pass through the neural network, making it efficient for localizing regions of interest.

<a id="item-408"></a>
## [GAP-GDRNet Boosts Spacecraft Pose Estimation with Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02360) ⭐️ 6.0/10

GAP-GDRNet introduces an attention-based feature refinement module and a patch-level geometric self-attention module into the GDR-Net pipeline, achieving 95.16% ADD@0.02m accuracy on a synthetic spacecraft dataset, outperforming the baseline by 3.88 percentage points. This work improves monocular 6D pose estimation for non-cooperative spacecraft, which is critical for autonomous rendezvous and on-orbit servicing. The attention enhancements address challenges like weak texture and occlusion, potentially enabling more reliable space operations. The method runs at 35.97 FPS for pose inference and also shows gains on general object datasets T-LESS and LM-O, with BOP AR improvements of 6.8 and 3.1 percentage points respectively. The synthetic dataset was built from a single CAD model using Blender.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 7, 04:00

**Background**: Monocular 6D pose estimation aims to determine the 3D rotation and translation of an object from a single RGB image. GDR-Net is a geometry-guided direct regression method that uses dense correspondences and a differentiable PnP layer called Patch-PnP. Spacecraft pose estimation is challenging due to weak texture, thin structures, and varying illumination.
